to complete the satisfaction of the linear hypotheses of the Nash-Moser theorem.

The main emphasis of this subsection is to prove the results in full detail in the omnisonic regime. We then, at the end, consider the completely analogous results in the subsonic regime. The proofs for these are then greatly abbreviated, as they are very similar to the omnisonic case except for a few minor distinctions.

We begin with the following definition of maps and function spaces, which are crucially utilized in the subsequent proofs. Throughout the rest of this subsection we will employ the notation set in Proposition
3.8
.

Definition 4.9

(Operators and adapted domains, omnisonic case)
.

Given
\gamma
,
\mu
,
\sigma
,
v_{0}
,
\eta_{0}
, and
\beta_{0}
as in Definition
4.1
, we define the following.

(1)

For
s\in\mathbb{N}
the map
L^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}:H^{2+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathcal{H}^{3+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to(\dot{H}^{-1}\cap H^{1+s})(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
is given by

(4.3.1)

L^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}(v,\eta)=P^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]+(0,\beta_{0}\nabla\eta).

(2)

For
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant 3
we define the space
{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}=\{{(v,\eta)\in{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\;:L_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}(v,\eta)\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}}\}
and equip it with the graph norm

(4.3.2)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}=\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}\mathbb{X}_{s}}}+\lVert L^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}.

We now come to the first part of the synthesis. The subscript
\mathrm{S}
stands for synthesis and the
s
refers to the data belonging to
\mathbb{Y}_{s}
.

Proposition 4.10

(Synthesis I, omnisonic case)
.

Let
(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)\in\mathcal{Q}
with
\mathcal{Q}
as in Definition
4.1
. For each
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}
there exists
\mathbb{R}^{+}\ni\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{S}s}\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{AP}s}
, depending only on
s
and
\mathcal{Q}
, such that if
\mathbb{R}^{+}\ni\varepsilon\in(0,\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{S}s}]
,
(v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0})
satisfy (
4.1.3
),
\upvarphi_{0}\in B_{H^{r}}(0,\varepsilon)\cap H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and
\uptau_{0}\in B_{H^{r}}(0,\varepsilon)\cap H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
, then the following hold.

(1)

The following linear map is well-defined and bounded:

(4.3.3)

{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\ni(v,\eta)\mapsto L^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}(v,\eta)+(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta)+(0,R^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta])\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}.

Moreover, this map is an isomorphism.

(2)

For all
(h,f)\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}
the unique
(v,\eta)\in{{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}
satisfying the system

(4.3.4)

L^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}(v,\eta)+(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta)+(0,R^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta])=(h,f)

obeys the tame estimate

(4.3.5)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert h,f\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0},\uptau_{0},\upvarphi_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{3+s}\times H^{3+s}\times H^{3+s}\times H^{3+s}}\lVert h,f\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}}},

where the implicit constant depends only on
s
,
d
, and
\mathcal{Q}
.

Proof.

We begin by verifying the map (
4.3.3
) is well-defined and bounded. Due to the definition of the norm on the space
{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}
, which is provided in Definition
4.9
, and the embedding
{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\hookrightarrow{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}
, it is sufficient to verify that the map

(4.3.6)

{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\ni(v,\eta)\mapsto\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta+R^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})

is well-defined and bounded. This is a consequence of the first item of Proposition
3.9
, Corollary
A.5
, and the definition of the norm on
{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}
from (
3.1.13
).

It remains to show that the map (
4.3.3
) is an isomorphism that obeys estimate (
4.3.5
). We will prove this in two steps. For the first step we shall use the following pair of definitions.

•

For
s\in\mathbb{N}
the map
M_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}:H^{2+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathcal{H}^{3+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to(\dot{H}^{-1}\cap H^{1+s})(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
is given via

(4.3.7)

M_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}(v,\eta)=Q^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]+(0,\beta_{0}\nabla\eta).

•

For
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant 3
we define the space
{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{s}=\{{(v,\eta)\in{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\;:\;M^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}(v,\eta)\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}}\}
and equip it with the graph norm

(4.3.8)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{s}}=\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}\mathbb{X}_{s}}}+\lVert M^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}.

The claim of the first step is then as follows. Let
b=\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}
. For each
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant b
there exists
\mathbb{R}^{+}\ni\rho_{\mathrm{XS}s}\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{AP}s}
with the property that for all
\mathbb{R}^{+}\ni\varepsilon\in(0,\rho_{\mathrm{XS}s}]
and
(h,f)\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}
there exists a unique
(v,\eta)\in{{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{s}}
satisfying

(4.3.9)

M^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}(v,\eta)+(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta)+(0,S^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v])=(h,f)

as well as the estimate

(4.3.10)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert h,f\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0},\uptau_{0},\upvarphi_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+s}\times H^{2+s}\times H^{2+s}\times H^{2+s}}\lVert h,f\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}}

for an implicit constant depending only on
s
,
d
, and
\mathcal{Q}
.

We begin the proof of the claim by considering the homotopy of operators
M_{t}:\;{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{s}\to\mathbb{Y}_{s}
defined for
t\in[0,1]
via

(4.3.11)

M_{t}(v,\eta)=M^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}(v,\eta)+t(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta)+t(0,S^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v]).

Thanks to the embedding
{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{s}\hookrightarrow\;{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}
and the remainder estimate of the second item of Proposition
3.9
, we readily deduce that each
M_{t}
is a well-defined and bounded linear map. Our goal is to use the method of continuity on the family
\{M_{t}\}_{t\in[0,1]}
, but to do so we must first establish the requisite estimates.

Provided that
\rho_{\mathrm{XS}s}\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{AP}s}
, the estimates of Theorem
4.8
grant us the bound

(4.3.12)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\lVert\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta+S^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v]\rVert_{H^{s}}\\
+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{1+s}\times H^{1+s}}(\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}+\lVert\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta+S^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v]\rVert_{H^{2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}).

We next take
s=b
in (
4.3.12
) and estimate

(4.3.13)

\lVert\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta\rVert_{H^{b}}\lesssim\varepsilon\lVert\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathcal{H}^{b}}\text{ and }\lVert S^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v]\rVert_{H^{b}}\lesssim\varepsilon\cdot{{}_{\mu}}\lVert v\rVert_{H^{b}},

by employing Corollary
A.5
for the former and the second item of Proposition
3.9
for the latter. Combining these with (
4.3.12
) shows that

(4.3.14)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{b}}\lesssim\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}}+\varepsilon\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{b}},

and thus if
\varepsilon\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{XS}b}
is taken sufficiently small we may use an absorbing argument to obtain the estimate

(4.3.15)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{b}}\lesssim\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}}.

Armed with (
4.3.15
), we return to (
4.3.12
) for
s\geqslant b
, but this time we estimate

(4.3.16)

\lVert\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta\rVert_{H^{s}}\lesssim\varepsilon\lVert\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathcal{H}^{s}}+\lVert\upvarphi_{0},\uptau_{0}\rVert_{H^{s}\times H^{s}}\lVert\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathcal{H}^{b}}\\
\text{and }\lVert S^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v]\rVert_{H^{s}}\lesssim\varepsilon\cdot{{}_{\mu}}\lVert v\rVert_{H^{s}}+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+s}}\lVert v\rVert_{H^{b}}

in order to deduce from (
4.3.12
), (
4.3.15
), and (
4.3.16
) that

(4.3.17)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0},\uptau_{0},\upvarphi_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+s}\times H^{2+s}\times H^{2+s}\times H^{2+s}}\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}}+\varepsilon\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}.

Further decreasing
\varepsilon\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{XS}s}\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{XS}b}
if necessary, we can again use an absorbing argument to see that

(4.3.18)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0},\uptau_{0}\upvarphi_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+s}\times H^{2+s}\times H^{2+s}\times H^{2+s}}\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}},

for an implicit constant depending only on
s
and
\mathcal{Q}
.

Estimate (
4.3.18
) is not quite what we need for the method of continuity, so we continue to work. To control the full norm on
{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{s}
it remains to estimate
\lVert M_{0}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}
. To this end, we first use (
4.3.11
) along with estimate (
4.3.16
):

(4.3.19)

\lVert M_{0}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}\leqslant\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\lVert(M_{0}-M_{t})(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}\\
+\varepsilon\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\upvarphi_{0},\uptau_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+s}\times H^{s}\times H^{s}}\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{b}}.

Next, we use (
4.3.15
) and (
4.3.18
) to handle the
(v,\eta)
terms on the right hand side of (
4.3.19
), which results in the bound

(4.3.20)

\lVert M_{0}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0},\uptau_{0}\upvarphi_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+s}\times H^{2+s}\times H^{2+s}\times H^{2+s}}\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}}.

Therefore, by combining (
4.3.18
) and (
4.3.20
) we obtain a constant
C\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending on
s
, the tuple
(v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0},\uptau_{0},\upvarphi_{0})
,
d
, and
\mathcal{Q}
but independent of
(v,\eta)
, such that

(4.3.21)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{s}}\leqslant C\lVert M_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}\text{ for all }t\in[0,1].

This, finally, is the estimate needed for the method of continuity. Theorem
4.8
shows that the operator
M_{0}
is an isomorphism, and so the bounds (
4.3.21
) and the the method of continuity establish that the operator
M_{1}
is also an isomorphism. Thus, the existence and uniqueness of solutions to (
4.3.9
) is established. The claimed estimate (
4.3.10
) then follows from (
4.3.18
), and this concludes the first step of the proof.

In the second step we aim to build on the result of the first step to complete the proof, and so we will actually seek
\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{S}s}\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{XS}s}
in order to justify use of the first step. Once more we will proceed via the method of continuity by considering the operator homotopy
L_{t}:{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\to\mathbb{Y}_{s}
defined for
t\in[0,1]
via

(4.3.22)

L_{t}(v,\eta)=M_{1}((1+\eta_{0})v,\eta)+t(0,R^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]).

Thanks to the embedding
{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\hookrightarrow\;{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}
, the remainder estimate in the first item of Proposition
3.9
, and the identity

(4.3.23)

M_{1}((1+\eta_{0})v,\eta)=P^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]+(0,\beta_{0}\nabla\eta+\upvarphi_{0}\eta)=L^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}(v,\eta)+(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta),

which is a consequence of the second item of Proposition
3.8
, we have that the
L_{t}
are well-defined and bounded linear maps. As above, we now turn to the derivation of the estimates needed for the method of continuity.

The condition
\varepsilon\leqslant\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{S}s}\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{XS}s}
allows us to invoke estimate (
4.3.18
) to acquire the following bound for
s\geqslant b=\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}
:

(4.3.24)

\lVert(1+\eta_{0})v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert L_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\lVert R^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]\rVert_{H^{s}}\\
+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0},\uptau_{0},\upvarphi_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{2+s}\times H^{2+s}\times H^{2+s}\times H^{2+s}}(\lVert L_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}}+\lVert R^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]\rVert_{H^{b}}).

By taking
s=b
in (
4.3.24
), using
\lVert\eta_{0}v\rVert_{{{}_{\mu}}H^{b}}\lesssim\varepsilon\lVert v\rVert_{{{}_{\mu}}H^{b}}
, and invoking the remainder estimate (
3.3.11
), we find that
\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{b}}\lesssim\lVert L_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}}+\varepsilon\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{b}}
; and hence, if
\varepsilon\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{S}b}
is sufficiently small we have the bound

(4.3.25)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{b}}\lesssim\lVert L_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}}.

We now return to (
4.3.24
) with the base estimate (
4.3.25
) in hand; again invoking the remainder estimate (
3.3.11
) and using the bound
\lVert\eta_{0}v\rVert_{{{}_{\mu}}H^{s}}\lesssim\varepsilon\lVert v\rVert_{H^{s}}+\lVert\eta_{0}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2+s}}\lVert v\rVert_{H^{b}}
, together with (
4.3.25
), we find that if
\varepsilon\leqslant\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{S}s}\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{S}b}
is sufficiently small then

(4.3.26)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert L_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0},\uptau_{0},\upvarphi_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{3+s}\times H^{3+s}\times H^{3+s}\times H^{3+s}}\lVert L_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}}.

As in the proof of the first step, estimate (
4.3.26
) will lead us to the estimates we need for the operator homotopy
\{L_{t}\}_{t\in[0,1]}
with a bit more work. The remaining piece of the norm on
{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}
is
\lVert L_{0}(v,\eta)-(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}
. By the same strategy used in (
4.3.19
), we derive the estimate

(4.3.27)

\lVert L_{0}(v,\eta)-(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert L_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\varepsilon\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{}_{\mu,\sigma}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\\
+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0},\uptau_{0},\upvarphi_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{3+s}\times H^{3+s}\times H^{3+s}\times H^{3+s}}\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{b}}.

Combining (
4.3.26
) and (
4.3.27
) then provides a constant
C^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, depending only on
s
,
d
,
\mathcal{Q}
, and
(v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0},\upvarphi_{0})
, such that

(4.3.28)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\leqslant C^{\prime}\lVert L_{t}(v,\eta)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}\text{ for all }t\in[0,1].

This and the method of continuity show that in order to show that
L_{1}
is an isomorphism it suffices to prove that the map
L_{0}
is an isomorphism.

Estimate (
4.3.28
) proves the injectivity of
L_{0}
. For surjectivity we let
(h,f)\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}
. The first step of the proof show that the map
M_{1}
is an isomorphism, so there exists
(w,\eta)\in{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{s}
such that
M_{1}(w,\eta)=(h,f)
. We set
v=w/(1+\eta_{0})
and note that Lemma
3.7
and Corollary
A.5
guarantee the inclusion
v\in{{}_{\mu}}H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

(4.3.29)

L^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}(v,\eta)=(h,f)-(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu+\sigma)\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta)\in\mathbb{Y}_{s},

and hence
(v,\eta)\in{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}
and
L_{0}(v,\eta)=(h,f)
. This shows that
L_{0}
is surjective, and so
L_{1}
is an isomorphism. This establishes that the map (
4.3.3
) is an isomorphism; the estimate (
4.3.5
) follows from (
4.3.26
) with
t=1
.
∎

Corollary 4.11

(Synthesis II, omnisonic case)
.

Let
(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)\in\mathcal{Q}
with
\mathcal{Q}
as in Definition
4.1
. For each
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}
there exists
\mathbb{R}^{+}\ni\rho_{\mathrm{S}s}\leqslant\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{S}s}
, depending only on
s
and
\mathcal{Q}
, such that if
\varepsilon\in(0,\rho_{\mathrm{S}s}]
and

(4.3.30)

(v_{0},\eta_{0},\{{\tau_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in B_{\mathbb{X}_{r}\times\mathbb{W}_{r}}(0,\varepsilon)\cap(\mathbb{X}_{\infty}\times\mathbb{W}_{\infty})\text{ for }r=\max\{{6,5+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\},

then the following hold.

(1)

Upon setting

(4.3.31)

\uptau_{0}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}\eta_{0}^{i}\tau_{0i},\hskip 10.00002pt\beta_{0}=\mathrm{Tr}\uptau_{0},\text{ and }\upvarphi_{0}=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}i\eta_{0}^{i-1}(\tau_{0i}\nabla\eta_{0}+\varphi_{0i}),

the linear map (
4.3.3
) is well-defined, bounded, and an isomorphism.

(2)

For all
(h,f)\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}
the unique
(v,\eta)\in{{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}
satisfying the system (
4.3.4
) obeys the tame estimate

(4.3.32)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert h,f\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\{{\tau_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{3+s}\times\mathbb{W}_{3+s}}\lVert h,f\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}}},

where the implicit constant depends only on
s
,
d
, and
\mathcal{Q}
.

Proof.

Thanks to Corollary
A.5
and Proposition
A.23
, we make take
0<\varepsilon\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{S}s}
sufficiently small to guarantee that
\uptau_{0}\in B_{H^{r}}(0,\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{S}s})\cap H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})
,
\beta_{0}\in B_{H^{r}}(0,\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{S}s})\cap H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and
\upvarphi_{0}\in B_{H^{r}}(0,\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{S}s})\cap H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
, where the parameter
\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{S}s}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
is granted by Proposition
4.10
. Therefore, by invoking this latter result, we obtain the first item and estimate (
4.3.5
). To swap to the claimed estimate (
4.3.32
), one simply need note that another application of Corollary
A.5
and Proposition
A.23
permits one the bound

(4.3.33)

\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0},\uptau_{0},\upvarphi_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{3+s}\times H^{3+s}\times H^{3+s}\times H^{3+s}}\lesssim\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\{{\tau_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{3+s}\times\mathbb{W}_{3+s}}.

∎

Remark 4.12
.

We are free to assume that the sequence
\{{\rho_{\mathrm{S}s}}\}_{s=3+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}^{\infty}\subset(0,\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}]
generated by by Corollary
4.11
is nonincreasing.

We are now ready for the first of our main theorems of our linear analysis.

Theorem 4.13

(Synthesis III, omnisonic case)
.

Set
b=\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}
, and let
\overline{\Uppsi}:\mathbb{E}_{b}\to\mathbb{F}_{b-3}
be the map given in (
3.2.4
), which is well-defined and smooth in light of the first item of Theorem
3.6
. Let
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant b
and
\rho_{\mathrm{S}s}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be given by Corollary
4.11
. Suppose that
0<\varepsilon\leqslant\min\{\rho_{\mathrm{S}s},\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}\}
(recall that the latter is defined in Lemma
3.7
),
I\Subset\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
J,K\Subset\mathbb{R}
are bounded open intervals, and

(4.3.34)

\mathbf{p}_{0}=(\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0},\{{\tau_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},w_{0},\xi_{0},v_{0},\eta_{0})\in[I\times J\times K\times B_{\mathbb{W}_{r}}(0,\varepsilon)\times B_{\mathbb{X}_{r}\times\mathbb{X}_{r}}(0,\varepsilon)]\cap\mathbb{E}_{\infty},

where
r\in\mathbb{N}
is as in Definition
4.1
. We set
\beta_{0}=\mathrm{Tr}\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}\eta_{0}^{i}\tau_{0i}\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. The following hold.

(1)

The restriction of
D\overline{\Uppsi}(\mathbf{p}_{0})
to
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}\times\mathbb{X}_{s}\times[{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}]
is well-defined and takes values in
\mathbb{F}_{s}
, and the induced linear map

(4.3.35)

D\overline{\Uppsi}(\mathbf{p}_{0}):\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}\times\mathbb{X}_{s}\times[{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}]\to\mathbb{F}_{s}

is an isomorphism.

(2)

Assume that
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbb{F}_{s}
and
\mathbf{e}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}\times\mathbb{X}_{s}\times[{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}]
is defined via
\mathbf{e}=(D\overline{\Uppsi}(\mathbf{p}_{0}))^{-1}\mathbf{f}
. We then have the tame estimate

(4.3.36)

\lVert\mathbf{e}\rVert_{\mathbb{E}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert\mathbf{f}\rVert_{\mathbb{F}_{s}}+\langle\lVert\mathbf{p}_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{E}_{s+3}}\rangle\lVert\mathbf{f}\rVert_{\mathbb{F}_{b}}

for an implicit constant depending only on
s
,
d
,
I
,
J
, and
K
.

Proof.

We break the proof into two steps. In the first step we use (
3.2.12
) to view
\Upupsilon:\mathbb{R}^{3}\times[\mathbb{X}_{b}\times\mathbb{W}_{b}]\to\mathbb{Y}_{b-3}\times\mathbb{W}_{b-3}
, defined in (
3.2.3
), as a smooth map with a domain comprised of two factors (namely,
\mathbb{R}^{3}
and
\mathbb{X}_{b}\times\mathbb{W}_{b}
) and study the derivative with respect to each factor evaluated at the point
\mathbf{q}_{0}=(\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0},v_{0},\eta_{0},\{{\tau_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})
. We begin with
D_{1}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0}):\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{Y}_{b-3}\times\mathbb{W}_{b-3}
. The inclusion (
3.2.12
) and the definition of strong
3-
tame smoothness (see Definition
A.9
) requires that

(4.3.37)

\lVert D_{1}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0})(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{t-3}\times\mathbb{W}_{t-3}}=\lVert D\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0})(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,0,0,0,0,0)\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{t-3}\times\mathbb{W}_{t-3}}\lesssim\langle\lVert\mathbf{q}_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{t}\times\mathbb{X}_{t}}\rangle\left\lVert\gamma,\mu,\sigma\right\rVert_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}

for every
t\geqslant b
, and so we actually have that

(4.3.38)

D_{1}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0})(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)\in\mathbb{Y}_{\infty}\times\mathbb{W}_{\infty}

for all
(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Next, we turn our attention to
D_{2}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0}):\mathbb{X}_{b}\times\mathbb{W}_{b}\to\mathbb{Y}_{b-3}\times\mathbb{W}_{b-3}
. By assumption, we have that
s\geqslant b
, and so
{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{X}_{b}
, which means that the space
{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}
lies within the domain of
D_{2}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0})
. We claim that for
s\geqslant b
and
\varepsilon\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{S}s}
we have the inclusion
D_{2}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0})({{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s})\subseteq\mathbb{Y}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}
and that the induced map

(4.3.39)

D_{2}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0}):{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}\to\mathbb{Y}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}.

is a bounded isomorphism obeying the tame bound

(4.3.40)

\lVert v,\eta,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}\rVert_{{{}_{\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert D_{2}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0})[v,\eta,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}]\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}}\\
+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\{{\tau_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{3+s}\times\mathbb{W}_{3+s}}\lVert D_{2}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0})[v,\eta,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}]\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}\times\mathbb{W}_{b}}.

We begin the proof of the claim by establishing the stated inclusion. Suppose that

(4.3.41)

D_{2}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0})[v,\eta,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}]=(h,f,\{{\widetilde{\tau}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\widetilde{\varphi}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})

for
(v,\eta,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}
.
Since
\Upupsilon
acts as the identity map on the
\mathbb{W}_{s}
factor, we have that

(4.3.42)

(\{{\widetilde{\tau}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\widetilde{\varphi}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})=(\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\mathbb{W}_{s}.

The remaining piece of (
4.3.41
) manifests as the equation

(4.3.43)

D_{2}\Uppsi(\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0},v_{0},\eta_{0})[v,\eta]+\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}(0,\eta_{0}^{i}(\mathrm{Tr}\tau_{0i}I+(\mu_{0}+\sigma_{0})\tau_{0i})\nabla\eta+i\eta_{0}^{i-1}(\tau_{0i}\nabla\eta_{0}+\varphi_{0i})\eta)\\
=(h,f)-\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}\eta_{0}^{i}(0,(\mathrm{Tr}\tau_{i}I+(\mu_{0}+\sigma_{0})\tau_{i})\nabla\eta_{0}+\varphi_{i}),

which, after defining
\uptau_{0}
,
\beta_{0}
, and
\upvarphi_{0}
as in (
4.3.31
) is equivalent to

(4.3.44)

L^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}(v,\eta)+(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+(\mu_{0}+\sigma_{0})\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta)+(0,R^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta])\\
=(h,f)-\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}\eta_{0}^{i}(0,(\mathrm{Tr}\tau_{i}I+(\mu_{0}+\sigma_{0})\tau_{i})\nabla\eta_{0}+\varphi_{i}),

thanks to the the first item of Proposition
3.8
and Definition
4.9
. Corollary
4.11
then guarantees that
(h,f)-\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}\eta_{0}^{i}(0,(\mathrm{Tr}\tau_{i}I+(\mu_{0}+\sigma_{0})\tau_{i})\nabla\eta_{0}+\varphi_{i})\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}
, from which it immediately follows that
(h,f)\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}
. Hence,
D_{2}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0})({{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s})\subseteq\mathbb{Y}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}
.

To complete the proof of the claim, it remains to show that (
4.3.39
) is an isomorphism obeying the estimate (
4.3.40
). Once more we consider the equation (
4.3.41
), but now with the aim of solving it for given data
(h,f,\{{\widetilde{\tau}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\widetilde{\varphi}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}
. The identity (
4.3.42
) remains true, and so again we reduce to (
4.3.44
). Applying Corollary
4.11
, we obtain the existence and uniqueness of
(v,\eta)\in{{}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}
satisfying (
4.3.44
). Furthermore, the proposition guarantees that the solution obeys the bound

(4.3.45)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\lesssim\Big\lVert(h,f)-\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}\eta_{0}^{i}(0,(\mathrm{Tr}\tau_{i}I+(\mu_{0}+\sigma_{0})\tau_{i})\nabla\eta_{0}+\varphi_{i})\Big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}\\
+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\{{\tau_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s+3}\times\mathbb{W}_{s+3}}\Big\lVert(h,f)-\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}\eta_{0}^{i}(0,(\mathrm{Tr}\tau_{i}I+(\mu_{0}+\sigma_{0})\tau_{i})\nabla\eta_{0}+\varphi_{i})\Big\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}},

but thanks to Corollary
A.5
and Proposition
A.23
, estimate (
4.3.45
) immediately provides the bound

(4.3.46)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert h,f,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}}\\
+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\{{\tau_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s+3}\times\mathbb{W}_{s+3}}\lVert h,f,\tau,\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\psi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{b}\times\mathbb{W}_{b}}.

As usual, the implicit constants here depend only on
s
,
d
,
I
,
J
, and
K
. Upon combining the above existence and uniqueness results, (
4.3.42
), and estimate (
4.3.46
) we then readily deduce that (
4.3.39
) is an isomorphism obeying the estimate (
4.3.40
), which completes the proof of the claim and concludes the first step of the proof.

We now move on to the second step of the proof, in which the goal is to attain the theorem statement by building on the results of the first step. Let
\mathbf{p}_{0}
be as in (
4.3.34
) and let
\mathbf{q}_{0}
be as in the first step. The system

(4.3.47)

D\overline{\Uppsi}(\mathbf{p}_{0})(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},w,\xi,v,\eta)=(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{\sigma},h,f,\{{\widetilde{\tau}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\widetilde{\varphi}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},u,\zeta)

is equivalent to the following system of four equations:

(4.3.48)

(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)=(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{\sigma}),

(4.3.49)

D_{1}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0})(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)+D_{2}\Upupsilon(\mathbf{q}_{0})(v,\eta,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})=(h,f,\{{\widetilde{\tau}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\widetilde{\varphi}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}),

(4.3.50)

w=\langle\mu_{0}\nabla\rangle^{2}v-u+2\mu\mu_{0}|\nabla|^{2}v_{0},

and

(4.3.51)

\xi=(1+(\mu_{0}+\sigma_{0})|\nabla|)\langle\sigma_{0}\nabla\rangle^{2}\eta-\zeta+(\mu+\sigma)|\nabla|\langle\sigma_{0}\nabla\rangle^{2}\eta_{0}+(1+(\mu_{0}+\sigma_{0})|\nabla|)\sigma\sigma_{0}|\nabla|^{2}\eta_{0}.

For brevity we denote in what follows

(4.3.52)

\mathbf{e}=(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},w,\xi,v,\eta)\text{ and }\mathbf{f}=(\widetilde{\gamma},\widetilde{\mu},\widetilde{\sigma},h,f,\{{\widetilde{\tau}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\widetilde{\varphi}_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},u,\zeta).

If
\mathbf{e}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}\times\mathbb{X}_{s}\times[{{}_{v_{0},\beta_{0},\eta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}]
, then we use the first step on (
4.3.49
) and trivial estimates on (
4.3.48
), (
4.3.50
), and (
4.3.51
) to see that
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbb{F}_{s}
and

(4.3.53)

\left\lVert\mathbf{f}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{F}_{s}}\lesssim\left\lVert\mathbf{e}\right\rVert_{\mathbb{W}_{s}\times\mathbb{X}_{s}\times[{{}_{v_{0},\beta_{0},\eta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}]}.

This shows that the linear map (
4.3.35
) is well-defined and bounded.

Now suppose that
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbb{F}_{s}
. We seek to find a unique
\mathbf{e}\in\mathbb{W}_{s}\times\mathbb{X}_{s}\times[{{}_{v_{0},\beta_{0},\eta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}]
solving (
4.3.47
). The identity (
4.3.48
) uniquely selects
(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)
, and then (
4.3.49
) and the work from the previous step uniquely selects
(v,\eta,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0},\beta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}
. From here it’s a simple matter to use (
4.3.50
) and (
4.3.51
) to uniquely select
(w,\xi)\in\mathbb{X}_{s}
. This process yields the desired
\mathbf{e}
and completes the proof of the first item. For the second item we employ (
4.3.40
) to see that the pair
(v,\eta)
determined by this process obeys the bound

(4.3.54)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert\gamma,\mu,\sigma,h,f,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}\rVert_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{Y}_{s}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}}\\
+\langle\lVert\mathbf{p}_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{E}_{s+3}}\rangle\lVert\gamma,\mu,\sigma,h,f,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}\rVert_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{Y}_{b}\times\mathbb{W}_{b}},

which, together with (
4.3.50
) and (
4.3.51
), then provides the estimate

(4.3.55)

\lVert w,\xi\rVert_{\mathbb{X}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert\mathbf{f}\rVert_{\mathbb{F}_{s}}+\langle\lVert\mathbf{p}_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{E}_{3+s}}\rangle\lVert\mathbf{f}\rVert_{\mathbb{F}_{b}}.

Combining these two estimates with the trivial bounds obtained from (
4.3.48
) then completes the proof of the second item.
∎

The remainder of this subsection is meant to provide subsonic analogs of the previous results. In the following definition, we recall that the space
{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}
is defined in (
3.1.13
) and the operator
P^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}
is given in the first item of Proposition
3.8
.

Definition 4.14

(Operators and adapted domains, subsonic case)
.

Given
\gamma
,
\mu
,
\sigma
,
v_{0}
, and
\eta_{0}
as in Definition
4.1
and
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant 3
we define the space
{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}=\{{(v,\eta)\in{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}}\;:\;P^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}}\}
and equip it with the graph norm

(4.3.56)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}=\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}+\lVert P^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}.

The following result is the subsonic refinement of Proposition
4.10
, as such the subscript
\mathrm{SS}
is an acronym meaning ‘subsonic synthesis’.

Proposition 4.15

(Synthesis I, subsonic case)
.

Let
(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)\in\mathcal{Q}
with
\mathcal{Q}=I\times J\times K
, as in Definition
4.1
, but suppose additionally that
I\Subset(0,1)
. For each
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}
there exists
\mathbb{R}^{+}\ni\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{SS}s}\leqslant\rho_{\mathrm{SAP}s}
, depending only on
s
and
\mathcal{Q}
, such that if
\mathbb{R}^{+}\ni\varepsilon\in(0,\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{SS}s}]
,

(4.3.57)

(v_{0},\eta_{0},\uptau_{0},\upvarphi_{0})\in B_{\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{r}\times H^{r}\times H^{r}}(0,\varepsilon)\cap(\mathbb{X}_{\infty}\times H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d})\times H^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})),

with
r=\max\{{6,5+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}
, then the following hold.

(1)

The following linear map is well-defined and bounded:

(4.3.58)

{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}\ni(v,\eta)\mapsto P^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]+(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta)+(0,R^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta])\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}.

Moreover, this map is an isomorphism.

(2)

For all
(h,f)\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}
the unique
(v,\eta)\in{{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}
satisfying the system

(4.3.59)

P^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]+(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta)+(0,R^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta])=(h,f)

obeys the tame estimate

(4.3.60)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert h,f\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\uptau_{0},\upvarphi_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{2+s}\times H^{2+s}\times H^{2+s}}\lVert h,f\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}}},

where the implicit constant depends only on
s
,
d
, and
\mathcal{Q}
.

Proof.

The proof exactly follows the strategy we employed to prove Proposition
4.10
, namely a double use of the method of continuity. As such, we will only provide a sketch of the argument here.

It is routine to verify that the map (
4.3.58
) is well-defined and bounded. In order to see that this map is an isomorphism obeying estimate (
4.3.60
), we again work in two steps. In the first step we apply the method continuity to an auxiliary homotopy of operators, which are defined as follows. For
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant 3
we define the space
{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}=\{{(v,\eta)\in{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}\;:\;Q^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}}\}
, where
Q^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}
is the operator from the second item of Proposition
3.8
, and equip it with the graph norm

(4.3.61)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\widetilde{\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}}=\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}+\lVert Q^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}.

The auxiliary mappings are then defined, for
t\in[0,1]
, to be

(4.3.62)

{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}}\widetilde{\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}\ni(v,\eta)\mapsto Q^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]+t(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta)+t(0,S^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v])\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}.

By combining the a priori estimates of Theorem
4.6
, the existence of Theorem
4.8
, and the remainder estimates of Proposition
3.9
we verify that the mappings (
4.3.62
) are well-defined and bounded and find
\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{SS}s}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
as in the proposition statement such that for
\varepsilon\leqslant\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{SS}s}
the maps (
4.3.62
) obey the requisite tame closed range estimates uniformly in
t\in[0,1]
. The method of continuity then applies, and we may indeed argue as in the first step of the proof of Proposition
4.10
.

The second step of the proof builds on the previous step by considering a different operator homotopy, namely

(4.3.63)

{{}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}\ni(v,\eta)\mapsto P^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta]+(0,\upvarphi_{0}\eta+\uptau_{0}\nabla\eta)+t(0,R^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}[v,\eta])\in\mathbb{Y}_{s},\hskip 10.00002ptt\in[0,1].

Supplementing the previous step with more remainder estimates from Proposition
3.9
allows us to take
\uprho_{\mathrm{SS}s}
smaller, if necessary, and deduce the claimed bounds (
4.3.60
) and also invoke the method of continuity on (
4.3.63
), leading us to a complete proof of the first and second items.
∎

Corollary 4.16

(Synthesis II, subsonic case)
.

Let
(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)\in\mathcal{Q}
with
\mathcal{Q}=I\times J\times K
, as in Definition
4.1
, but suppose additionally that
I\Subset(0,1)
. For each
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}
there exists
\mathbb{R}^{+}\ni\rho_{\mathrm{SS}s}\leqslant\widetilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{SS}s}
, depending only on
s
and
\mathcal{Q}
, such that if
\mathbb{R}^{+}\ni\varepsilon\in(0,\rho_{\mathrm{SS}s}]
and
(v_{0},\eta_{0},\{{\tau_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})
satisfy (
4.3.30
) then the following hold.

(1)

Upon defining
\uptau_{0}
,
\beta_{0}
, and
\upvarphi_{0}
as in (
4.3.31
), we have that the linear map (
4.3.58
) is well-defined, bounded, and an isomorphism.

(2)

For all
(h,f)\in\mathbb{Y}_{s}
the unique
(v,\eta)\in{{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}^{\gamma,\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}
satisfying (
4.3.59
) obeys the tame estimate

(4.3.64)

\lVert v,\eta\rVert_{{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert h,f\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{s}}+\lVert v_{0},\eta_{0},\{{\tau_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell}\rVert_{\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{2+s}\times\mathbb{W}_{2+s}}\lVert h,f\rVert_{\mathbb{Y}_{\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}}},

where the implicit constant depends only on
s
,
d
, and
\mathcal{Q}
.

Proof.

We derive these claims from Proposition (
4.15
) by arguing as in the proof of Corollary
4.11
.
∎

Theorem 4.17

(Synthesis III, subsonic case)
.

Set
b=\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}
, and let
\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}:\mathbb{E}_{b}\to\mathbb{F}_{b-2}
be the map given in (
3.2.7
), which is well-defined and smooth in light of the second item of Theorem
3.6
. Let
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant b
and
\rho_{\mathrm{SS}s}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
be given by Corollary
4.16
. Suppose that
0<\varepsilon\leqslant\min\{\rho_{\mathrm{SS}s},\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}\}
(recall that the latter is defined in Lemma
3.7
),
I\Subset(0,1)
and
J,K\Subset\mathbb{R}
are bounded open intervals, and

(4.3.65)

\mathbf{p}_{0}=(\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0},\{{\tau_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{0i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},w_{0},\xi_{0},v_{0},\eta_{0})\in[I\times J\times K\times B_{\mathbb{W}_{r}}(0,\varepsilon)\times B_{\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{r}\times\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{r}}(0,\varepsilon)]\cap\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\infty},

where
r=\max\{{6,5+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}
. The following hold.

(1)

The restriction of
D\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}(\mathbf{p}_{0})
to
\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}\times\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}\times[{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}]
is well-defined and takes values in
\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}
, and the induced linear map

(4.3.66)

D\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}(\mathbf{p}_{0}):\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}\times\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}\times[{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}]\to\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}

is an isomorphism.

(2)

Assume that
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}
and
\mathbf{e}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{s}\times\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}\times[{{}_{v_{0},\eta_{0}}^{\gamma_{0},\mu_{0},\sigma_{0}}}\mathbb{X}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}]
is defined via
\mathbf{e}=(D\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}(\mathbf{p}_{0}))^{-1}\mathbf{f}
. We then have the tame estimate

(4.3.67)

\lVert\mathbf{e}\rVert_{\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}\lesssim\lVert\mathbf{f}\rVert_{\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}+\langle\lVert\mathbf{p}_{0}\rVert_{\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s+2}}\rangle\lVert\mathbf{f}\rVert_{\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{b}}

for an implicit constant depending only on
s
,
d
,
I
,
J
, and
K
.

Proof.

The same strategy used in the proof of Theorem
4.13
applies here, with minor modifications; for instance, instead of the omnisonic results such as Corollary
4.11
, we instead invoke the corresponding subsonic result from Corollary
4.16
. In the interest of brevity, we omit further details.
∎

5.
Conclusion

In this section we piece together the nonlinear analysis of Section
3
and the linear analysis of Section
4
along with the Nash-Moser inverse function theorem of Appendix
A.3
to prove Theorems
3
and
4
along with Corollaries
5.3
and
5.4
.

5.1.
Abstract construction

In the following first formulations of the main theorem for the omnisonic and subsonic cases, we will utilize the following variations on the main nonlinear operators
\overline{\Uppsi}
and
\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}
, which we recall are defined in (
3.2.4
) and (
3.2.7
), respectively. For
b=\max\{{3,2+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}\}\in\mathbb{N}
and any fixed
\mathfrak{q}=(\overline{\gamma},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
we define the operators
\overline{\Uppsi}_{\mathfrak{q}}:\mathbb{E}_{b}\to\mathbb{F}_{b-3}
and
\overline{\Uppsi}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\mathrm{sub}}:\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{b}\to\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{b-2}
via the assignments

(5.1.1)

\overline{\Uppsi}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},w,\xi,v,\eta)=\overline{\Uppsi}(\gamma+\overline{\gamma},\mu+\overline{\mu},\sigma+\overline{\sigma},\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},w,\xi,v,\eta)-(\overline{\gamma},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma},0,0,0)

and

(5.1.2)

\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},w,\xi,v,\eta)=\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}(\gamma+\overline{\gamma},\mu+\overline{\mu},\sigma+\overline{\sigma},\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},w,\xi,v,\eta)-(\overline{\gamma},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma},0,0,0)

The utility of these definitions is that
\overline{\Uppsi}_{\mathfrak{q}}(0)=\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\mathfrak{q}}(0)=0
.

Theorem 5.1

(Abstract formulation, omnisonic case)
.

Let
\varsigma=19+2\lfloor d/2\rfloor
. For each
\mathfrak{q}=(\overline{\gamma},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, there exists a nonincreasing sequence
\{{\uprho_{s}(\mathfrak{q})}\}_{s=0}^{\infty}\subset(0,\min\{{\rho_{\mathrm{WD}},\overline{\gamma}/2}\}]
and
\upkappa(\mathfrak{q})\in\mathbb{R}^{+}

with the following properties.

(1)

Existence and uniqueness:
Given
\mathbf{f}\in B_{\mathbb{F}_{\varsigma}}(0,\uprho_{0}(\mathfrak{q}))
there exists a unique
\mathbf{e}\in B_{\mathbb{E}_{\varsigma}}(0,\upkappa(\mathfrak{q})\uprho_{0}(\mathfrak{q}))
such that
\overline{\Uppsi}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\mathbf{e})=\mathbf{f}
. This induces the local inverse map

(5.1.3)

\overline{\Uppsi}^{-1}_{\mathfrak{q}}:B_{\mathbb{F}_{\varsigma}}(0,\uprho_{0}(\mathfrak{q}))\to\overline{\Uppsi}^{-1}_{\mathfrak{q}}(B_{\mathbb{F}_{\varsigma}}(0,\uprho_{0}(\mathfrak{q})))\subseteq B_{\mathbb{E}_{\varsigma}}(0,\upkappa(\mathfrak{q})\uprho_{0}(\mathfrak{q})).

(2)

Higher regularity, given low norm smallness:
If
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbb{F}_{s+\varsigma}\cap B_{\mathbb{F}_{\varsigma}}(0,\uprho_{s}(\mathfrak{q}))
, then we have the inclusion
\overline{\Uppsi}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1}(\mathbf{f})\in\mathbb{E}_{s+\varsigma}
and the tame estimate

(5.1.4)

\lVert\overline{\Uppsi}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1}(\mathbf{f})\rVert_{\mathbb{E}_{\varsigma+s}}\lesssim\lVert\mathbf{f}\rVert_{\mathbb{F}_{\varsigma+s}},

for an implicit constant depending only on
s
, the dimension, and
\mathfrak{q}
.

(3)

Continuous dependence:
For every
s\in\mathbb{N}
, the restricted map

(5.1.5)

\overline{\Uppsi}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1}:\mathbb{F}_{s+\varsigma}\cap B_{\mathbb{F}_{\varsigma}}(0,\uprho_{s}(\mathfrak{q}))\to\mathbb{E}_{\varsigma+s}\cap B_{\mathbb{E}_{\varsigma}}(0,\upkappa(\mathfrak{q})\uprho_{0}(\mathfrak{q}))

is continuous with respect to the norms on
\mathbb{F}_{s+\varsigma}
and
\mathbb{E}_{s+\varsigma}
.

Proof.

Our aim is to show that the hypotheses of Theorem
A.19
are satisfied by the map
\overline{\Uppsi}_{\mathfrak{q}}
, defined in (
5.1.1
), acting between the Banach scales
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{E}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{F}}
, which we recall are defined in (
3.1.7
). Evidently, these Banach scales consist of reflexive (in fact, Hilbert) spaces and, thanks to Lemma
3.1
, we know that
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{E}}
and
\boldsymbol{\mathbb{F}}
are LP-smoothable in the sense of Definition
A.15
.

We now assess how the triple
(\boldsymbol{\mathbb{E}},\boldsymbol{\mathbb{F}},\overline{\Uppsi}_{\mathfrak{q}})
satisfies the LRI mapping hypotheses of Definition
A.18
with parameters

(5.1.6)

(\mu,r,R)=(3,6+\lfloor d/2\rfloor,33+3\lfloor d/2\rfloor),

which obey the inequalities
1\leqslant\mu\leqslant r<R<\infty
and
2(r+\mu)+1<(r+R)/2
as well as the identity
\varsigma=2(r+\mu)+1
. We then set
\delta_{r}=\min\{{\rho_{\mathrm{WD}},\overline{\gamma}/2}\}
. By heeding to these definitions, Theorem
3.6
and Corollary
A.12
, we see that the first and second items of Definition
A.18
are satisfied. The satisfaction of the third and final item of Definition
A.18
is a consequence of Theorem
4.13
and Remark
4.12
with
\delta_{R}=\min\{{\delta_{r},\rho_{\mathrm{S}R}}\}
(the parameter
\rho_{\mathrm{S}R}
is granted by the theorem) and
I=(\overline{\gamma}/2,2\overline{\gamma})
,
J=(-2|\overline{\mu}|,2|\overline{\mu}|)
, and
K=(-2|\overline{\sigma}|,2|\overline{\sigma}|)
.

Thus, the hypotheses of Theorem
A.19
are satisfied and we therefore obtain
\varepsilon=\uprho_{0}(\mathfrak{q})
such that the first item holds, the map (
5.1.3
) is continuous, and we have the estimate
\lVert\overline{\Uppsi}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{-1}(\mathbf{f})\rVert_{\mathbb{E}_{\varsigma}}\lesssim\lVert\mathbf{f}\rVert_{\mathbb{F}_{\varsigma}}
for
\mathbf{f}\in B_{\mathbb{F}_{\varsigma}}(0,\uprho_{0}(\mathfrak{q}))
.

Finally, suppose that
s\in\mathbb{N}
. We again apply the Nash-Moser theorem, but this time with the parameter triple
(\mu,r,\widetilde{R})=(\mu,r,R+s)
and
\delta_{\widetilde{R}}=\min\{{\delta_{R},\rho_{\mathrm{S}\widetilde{R}}}\}
and let
\uprho_{s}(\mathfrak{q})\in(0,\rho_{\mathrm{WD}}]
denote the smallness parameter
\varepsilon
provided by this application of Theorem
A.19
. By local uniqueness, we are thus granted the second and third items.
∎

We are now interested in the subsonic sharpening of Theorem
5.1
, which we state and prove below.

Theorem 5.2

(Abstract formulation, subsonic case)
.

Let
\varpi=17+2\lfloor d/2\rfloor
. For each
\mathfrak{q}=(\overline{\gamma},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})\in(0,1)\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, there exists nonincreasing
\{{\uprho^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}(\mathfrak{q})}\}_{s=0}^{\infty}\subset(0,\min\{{\rho_{\mathrm{WD}},\overline{\gamma}/2,(1-\overline{\gamma})2}\}]
and
\upkappa^{\mathrm{sub}}(\mathfrak{q})\in\mathbb{R}^{+}

with the following properties.

(1)

Existence and uniqueness:
For
\mathbf{f}\in B_{\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\varpi}}(0,\uprho^{\mathrm{sub}}_{0}(\mathfrak{q}))
there exists a unique
\mathbf{e}\in B_{\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\varpi}}(0,\upkappa^{\mathrm{sub}}(\mathfrak{q})\uprho^{\mathrm{sub}}_{0}(\mathfrak{q}))
such that
\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\mathfrak{q}}(\mathbf{e})=\mathbf{f}
. This induces the local inverse map

(5.1.7)

[\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\mathfrak{q}}]^{-1}:B_{\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\varpi}}(0,\uprho^{\mathrm{sub}}_{0}(\mathfrak{q}))\to[\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\mathfrak{q}}]^{-1}(B_{\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\varpi}}(0,\uprho^{\mathrm{sub}}_{0}(\mathfrak{q})))\subseteq B_{\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\varpi}}(0,\upkappa^{\mathrm{sub}}(\mathfrak{q})\uprho^{\mathrm{sub}}_{0}(\mathfrak{q})).

(2)

Higher regularity, given low norm smallness:
If
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s+\varpi}\cap B_{\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\varpi}}(0,\uprho^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}(\mathfrak{q}))
, then we have the inclusion
[\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\mathfrak{q}}]^{-1}(\mathbf{f})\in\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s+\varpi}
and the tame estimate

(5.1.8)

\lVert[\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\mathfrak{q}}]^{-1}(\mathbf{f})\rVert_{\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\varpi+s}}\lesssim\lVert\mathbf{f}\rVert_{\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\varpi+s}},

for an implicit constant depending only on
s
, the dimension, and
\mathfrak{q}
.

(3)

Continuous dependence:
For every
s\in\mathbb{N}
, the restricted map

(5.1.9)

[\overline{\Uppsi}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{\mathrm{sub}}]^{-1}:\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s+\varpi}\cap B_{\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\varpi}}(0,\uprho^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}(\mathfrak{q}))\to\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\varpi+s}\cap B_{\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\varpi}}(0,\upkappa^{\mathrm{sub}}(\mathfrak{q})\uprho^{\mathrm{sub}}_{0}(\mathfrak{q}))

is continuous with respect to the norms on
\mathbb{F}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s+\varpi}
and
\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s+\varpi}
.

Proof.

The proof is much the same as that of Theorem
5.1
; the main differences are that we use the second item of Theorem
3.6
in place of the first and Theorem
4.17
instead of Theorem
4.13
. The reason for the difference between
\varsigma
and
\varpi
is because the map
\overline{\Uppsi}
is
3
-tame, while
\overline{\Uppsi}^{\mathrm{sub}}
is only
2
-tame. We omit further details.
∎

5.2.
PDE construction

The goal of this subsection is to take the abstract formulations of Theorems
5.1
and
5.2
and transform them into Theorems
3
and
4
along with a few corollaries.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

For each
\mathfrak{q}=(\overline{\gamma},\overline{\mu},\overline{\sigma})\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times[0,\infty)^{2}
we may invoke Theorem
5.1
and acquire a nonincreasing sequence
\{{\uprho_{\mathfrak{s}}(\mathfrak{q})}\}_{\mathfrak{s}=0}^{\infty}\subset(0,\min\{{\rho_{\mathrm{WD}},\overline{\gamma}/2}\}]
and
\upkappa(\mathfrak{q})\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that the various conclusions of the theorem hold. We then define the open and relatively open sets

(5.2.1)

\mathcal{V}(\mathfrak{q})=B_{\mathbb{X}_{\varsigma}}(0,\upkappa(\mathfrak{q})\uprho_{0}(\mathfrak{q}))\subset\mathbb{X}_{\varsigma}.

and, for
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\varsigma
,

(5.2.2)

\mathcal{U}_{s}=\Big\{{(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\bigcup_{\mathfrak{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times[0,\infty)^{2}}B_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{\varsigma}}((\mathfrak{q},0),\uprho_{s-\varsigma}(\mathfrak{q}))\;:\mu\geqslant 0,\;\sigma\geqslant 0}\Big\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{\varsigma}.

The first properties of
\mathcal{U}_{s}
and
\mathcal{V}(\mathfrak{q})
in (
1.3.9
) and (
1.3.10
) are now clear.

For the first item, we apply the first conclusion of Theorem
5.1
. Indeed, given
(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\mathcal{U}_{\varsigma}
we define
(v,\eta)\in\mathcal{V}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)
to be the last two components of the tuple
\overline{\Uppsi}_{(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)}^{-1}(0,0,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},0,0)
. This grants us existence and uniqueness, and hence the second item is proved.

It remains to prove the second and third items, which we do in tandem. It is clear from Theorem
5.1
that, given
(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\mathcal{U}_{s}\cap(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{s})
, the solution
(v,\eta)
belongs also to
\mathbb{X}_{s}
and the map

(5.2.3)

\mathcal{U}_{s}\cap(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{s})\ni(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\mapsto(v,\eta)\in\mathbb{X}_{s}

is continuous. It is at this point where we crucially utilize the last two components of the mapping
\overline{\Uppsi}
from (
3.2.4
). Indeed, we have that
\overline{\Uppsi}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},w,\xi,v,\eta)=(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,0,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},0,0)
and, by arguments similar to the above, the map

(5.2.4)

\mathcal{U}_{s}\cap(\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{s})\ni(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\mapsto(w,\xi)\in\mathbb{X}_{s}

is also continuous; however, we have the identities

(5.2.5)

w=\langle\mu\nabla\rangle^{2}v\hskip 10.00002pt\text{and}\hskip 10.00002pt\xi=(1+(\mu+\sigma)|\nabla|)\langle\sigma\nabla\rangle^{2}\eta

which, in turn, lead to the inclusion
(v,\eta)\in{{}_{\mu,\sigma}}\mathbb{X}_{s}
(which implies (
1.3.12
)) and the continuity of the maps (
1.3.13
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
4
.

The proof is very similar to the proof of Theorem
3
; the main difference is that we use Theorem
5.2
in place of Theorem
5.1
. We omit further details for the sake of brevity.
∎

We now shall enumerate some important consequences of Theorems
3
and
4
.

Corollary 5.3

(Well-posedness for fixed physical parameters)
.

Let
(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times[0,\infty)^{2}
. The following hold.

(1)

There exists a nonincreasing sequence of open sets

(5.2.6)

\{{0}\}^{\ell+1}\times\{{0}\}^{\ell+1}\subset\mathcal{U}_{s}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)\subset(H^{\varsigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}))^{\ell+1}\times(H^{\varsigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\ell+1},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\varsigma

such that the following hold.

(a)

For all
(\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\mathcal{U}_{\varsigma}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)
there exists a unique
(v,\eta)\in\mathcal{V}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)
such that system (
1.1.9
) is classically satisfied by the latter tuple with physical parameters
(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)
and data
\mathcal{F}
determined from
\eta
,
\mu+\sigma
, and
(\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})
via the omnisonic case of (
1.1.12
).

(b)

If
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\varsigma
and
(\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\mathcal{U}_{s}\cap((H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}))^{\ell+1}\times(H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\ell+1})

then the corresponding solution
(v,\eta)\in\mathcal{V}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)
produced by the previous item in fact obeys the inclusions
\langle\mu\nabla\rangle^{2}v\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
(1+(\mu+\sigma)|\nabla|)\langle\sigma\nabla\rangle^{2}\eta\in\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
; moreover the mapping from the data to the solution is continuous for the spaces determined via this inclusion.

(2)

If we assume additionally that
\gamma<1
, then there exists another nonincreasing sequence of open sets

(5.2.7)

\{{0}\}^{\ell+1}\times\{{0}\}^{\ell+1}\subset\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)\subset(H^{\varpi}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}))^{\ell+1}\times(H^{\varpi}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\ell+1},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\varpi

such that the following hold.

(a)

For all
(\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{\varpi}
there exists a unique
(v,\eta)\in\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{sub}}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)
such that system (
1.1.9
) is classically satisfied by the latter tuple with physical parameters
(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)
and data
\mathcal{F}
determined from
\eta
and
(\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})
via the subsonic case of (
1.1.12
).

(b)

If
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\varpi
and
(\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}\cap((H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}))^{\ell+1}\times(H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\ell+1})
then the corresponding solution
(v,\eta)\in\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{sub}}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)
produced by the previous item in fact obeys the inclusions
\langle\mu\nabla\rangle^{2}v\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\langle\sigma\nabla\rangle^{2}\eta\in\mathcal{H}^{1+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
; moreover the mapping from the data to the solution is continuous for the spaces determined via this inclusion.

Proof.

Throughout the proof we shall let
\mathrm{int}
denote the topological interior. We begin by defining the open sets of (
5.2.6
) and (
5.2.7
); we set:

(5.2.8)

\mathcal{U}_{s}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)=\mathrm{int}\{{(\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\;:\;(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\mathcal{U}_{s}}\},

and, if
\gamma<1
, similarly define

(5.2.9)

\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)=\mathrm{int}\{{(\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\;:\;(\gamma,\mu,\sigma,\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}}\}.

The sets in (
5.2.8
) and (
5.2.9
) are manifestly open and nonempty since due to the fact that
\mathcal{U}_{s}
and
\mathcal{U}_{s}^{\mathrm{sub}}
are are relatively open and satisfy the nondegeneracy conditions of Theorems
3
and
4
, respectively. With these open sets in hand, we see that the first and second items of above are just restrictions of the conclusions of Theorems
3
and
4
to the slices defined above.
∎

Corollary 5.4

(On the limits of vanishing viscosity and surface tension)
.

Let
\gamma,R_{\mathrm{visc}},R_{\mathrm{surf}}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. The following hold.

(1)

There exists a nonincreasing sequence of open sets

(5.2.10)

\{{0}\}^{\ell+1}\times\{{0}\}^{\ell+1}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{s}(\gamma,R_{\mathrm{visc}},R_{\mathrm{surf}})\subset(H^{\varsigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}))^{\ell+1}\times(H^{\varsigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\ell+1},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\varsigma

such that the following hold.

(a)

We have the inclusion
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{s}(\gamma,R_{\mathrm{visc}},R_{\mathrm{surf}})\subseteq\bigcap_{0\leqslant\mu\leqslant R_{\mathrm{visc}}}\bigcap_{0\leqslant\sigma\leqslant R_{\mathrm{surf}}}\mathcal{U}_{s}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)
.

(b)

For each fixed
(\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{s}(\gamma,R_{\mathrm{visc}},R_{\mathrm{surf}})\cap(H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}))^{\ell+1}\times(H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\ell+1})
, where
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\varsigma
, there exists a continuous mapping

(5.2.11)

[0,R_{\mathrm{visc}}]\times[0,R_{\mathrm{surf}}]\ni(\mu,\sigma)\mapsto(v,\eta)\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})

where
(v,\eta)
solve (
1.1.9
) as in Theorem
3
.

(c)

The following derivatives of each mapping in (
5.2.11
) are well-defined and continuous between the stated spaces:

(5.2.12)

[0,R_{\mathrm{visc}}]\times[0,R_{\mathrm{surf}}]\ni(\mu,\sigma)\mapsto(\mu\nabla v,\mu^{2}\nabla^{2}v)\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}})\times H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d^{3}})

and

(5.2.13)

[0,R_{\mathrm{visc}}]\times[0,R_{\mathrm{surf}}]\ni(\mu,\sigma)\mapsto(\mu+\sigma)(\nabla\eta,\sigma\nabla^{2}\eta,\sigma^{2}\nabla^{3}\eta)\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})\times H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}})\times H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d^{3}}).

(2)

If we assume additionally that
\gamma<1
, then there exists an additional nonincreasing sequence of open sets

(5.2.14)

\{{0}\}^{\ell+1}\times\{{0}\}^{\ell+1}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}(\gamma,R_{\mathrm{visc}},R_{\mathrm{surf}})\subset(H^{\varpi}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}))^{\ell+1}\times(H^{\varpi}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\ell+1},\hskip 10.00002pt\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\varpi

such that the following hold.

(a)

We have the inclusion
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}(\gamma,R_{\mathrm{visc}},R_{\mathrm{surf}})\subseteq\bigcap_{0\leqslant\mu\leqslant R_{\mathrm{visc}}}\bigcap_{0\leqslant\sigma\leqslant R_{\mathrm{surf}}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)
.

(b)

For each
(\{{\tau_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell},\{{\varphi_{i}}\}_{i=0}^{\ell})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}(\gamma,R_{\mathrm{visc}},R_{\mathrm{surf}})\cap(H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}))^{\ell+1}\times(H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d}))^{\ell+1})
, where
\mathbb{N}\ni s\geqslant\varpi
, there exists a continuous mapping

(5.2.15)

[0,R_{\mathrm{visc}}]\times[0,R_{\mathrm{surf}}]\ni(\mu,\sigma)\mapsto(v,\eta)\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathcal{H}^{1+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})

where
(v,\eta)
solve (
1.1.9
) as in Theorem
4
.

(c)

The following derivatives of each mapping in (
5.2.15
) are well-defined and continuous between the stated spaces:

(5.2.16)

[0,R_{\mathrm{visc}}]\times[0,R_{\mathrm{surf}}]\ni(\mu,\sigma)\mapsto(\mu\nabla v,\mu^{2}\nabla^{2}v)\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}})\times H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d^{3}})

and

(5.2.17)

[0,R_{\mathrm{visc}}]\times[0,R_{\mathrm{surf}}]\ni(\mu,\sigma)\mapsto(\sigma\nabla\eta,\sigma^{2}\nabla^{2}\eta)\in H^{1+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})\times H^{1+s}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}).

Proof.

Given
\gamma
,
R_{\mathrm{visc}}
, and
R_{\mathrm{surf}}
as in the statement of the corollary, we define

(5.2.18)

\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{s}(\gamma,R_{\mathrm{visc}},R_{\mathrm{surf}})=\mathrm{int}\bigcap_{0\leqslant\mu\leqslant R_{\mathrm{visc}}}\bigcap_{0\leqslant\sigma\leqslant R_{\mathrm{surf}}}\mathcal{U}_{s}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma)

and, provided
\gamma<1
,

(5.2.19)

\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}(\gamma,R_{\mathrm{visc}},R_{\mathrm{surf}})=\mathrm{int}\bigcap_{0\leqslant\mu\leqslant R_{\mathrm{visc}}}\bigcap_{0\leqslant\sigma\leqslant R_{\mathrm{surf}}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}(\gamma,\mu,\sigma),

where again we use
\mathrm{int}
to denote the topological interior. We note that the set
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{s}(\gamma,R_{\mathrm{visc}},R_{\mathrm{surf}})
is indeed non-empty since inspection of the definition of the set
\mathcal{U}_{s}
in (
5.2.2
) allows one to conclude that

(5.2.20)

\mathrm{dist}\Big(\{{\gamma}\}\times[0,R_{\mathrm{visc}}]\times[0,R_{\mathrm{surf}}]\times\{{0}\},\partial\Big(\bigcup_{\mathfrak{q}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times[0,\infty)^{2}}B_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{W}_{\varsigma}}((\mathfrak{q},0),\uprho_{s-\varsigma}(\mathfrak{q}))\Big)\Big)>0.

Hence
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{s}(\gamma,R_{\mathrm{visc}},R_{\mathrm{surf}})
contains balls of positive radii of length at most the above distance. A similar argument verifies that
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{sub}}_{s}(\gamma,R_{\mathrm{visc}},R_{\mathrm{surf}})
is also non-empty. Armed with the open sets of (
5.2.18
) and (
5.2.19
), we find that the conclusions stated above are immediate consequences of Theorems
3
and
4
.
∎

Appendix A
Analysis background

A.1.
On the anisotropic Sobolev spaces

For
\mathbb{R}\ni s\geqslant 0
and
d\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
we define the anisotropic Sobolev space

(A.1.1)

\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\{{f\in\mathscr{S}^{\ast}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})\;:\;\mathscr{F}[f]\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{C}),\;\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}<\infty}\}{\@index{\@indexphrase{{Function spaces}}\@indexphrase[170]{$\mathcal{H}^{s}$}}},

equipped with the norm

(A.1.2)

\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}=\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big(|\xi|^{-2}(\xi_{1}^{2}+|\xi|^{4})\mathds{1}_{B(0,1)}(\xi)+\langle\xi\rangle^{2s}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B(0,1)}(\xi)\big)|\mathscr{F}[f](\xi)|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\xi\Big)^{1/2}.

These spaces were introduced in Leoni and Tice
[
38
]
, where it was shown, in Proposition 5.3 and Theorem 5.6, that
\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is a Hilbert space and
H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})+C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, with equality in the first embedding if and only if
d=1
.

We shall find the following characterizations useful.

Proposition A.1

(Characterizations of the anisotropic Sobolev spaces)
.

The following hold for
\mathbb{R}\ni s\geqslant 0
.

(1)

If
f\in\mathscr{S}^{\ast}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R})
satisfies
\nabla f\in H^{s-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\partial_{1}f\in\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then there exists a constant
c\in\mathbb{R}
such that
f-c\in\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

(2)

Assume
d\geqslant 2
and write
p_{d}=\frac{2(d+1)}{d-1}>2
. Then we have the equality

(A.1.3)

\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\Big\{{f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})+\bigcup_{p_{d}<p<\infty}L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\;:\;\nabla f\in H^{s-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{ and }\partial_{1}f\in\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big\},

with the equivalence of norms

(A.1.4)

\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\asymp\sqrt{\lVert\nabla f\rVert_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+[{\partial_{1}f}]_{\dot{H}^{-1}}^{2}},

where the implied constants depend only on
d
and
s
.

(3)

With
d
and
p_{d}
as in the previous item, we have the equality

(A.1.5)

\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\Big\{{f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})+\bigcup_{p_{d}<p<\infty}L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\;:\;\nabla f\in H^{s-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{ and }\mathcal{R}_{1}f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big\}

with the equivalence of norms

(A.1.6)

\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\asymp\sqrt{\lVert\nabla f\rVert_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\lVert\mathcal{R}_{1}f\rVert_{L^{2}}^{2}},

where
\mathcal{R}_{1}
is the Riesz transform in the
e_{1}
-direction.

Proof.

The first and second items are the content of Proposition B.4 in Stevenson and Tice
[
56
]
. That the third item holds follows from the fact that
\partial_{1}f\in\dot{H}^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is equivalent to
\mathcal{R}_{1}f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for any
f\in(L^{2}+L^{q})(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for
p_{d}<q<\infty
.
∎

The following result is Lemma B.6 in Stevenson and Tice
[
56
]
.

Proposition A.2

(LP-smoothability)
.

The Banach scale
\{{\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\}_{s\in\mathbb{N}}
is LP-smoothable in the sense of Definition
A.15
.

We next discuss a high-low decomposition for which the following notational convention is set. For
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
, we define the linear operators
\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}}
and
\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{H}}
on the subspace of
f\in\mathscr{S}^{\ast}(\mathbb{R}^{d};\mathbb{C})
such that
\mathscr{F}[f]
is locally integrable via

(A.1.7)

\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}}f=\mathscr{F}^{-1}[\mathds{1}_{B(0,\kappa)}\mathscr{F}[f]]\text{ and
}\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{H}}f=(I-\Uppi^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{L}})f.

Proposition A.3

(Frequency splitting for anisotropic Sobolev spaces)
.

The following hold for
0\leqslant s\in\mathbb{R}
,
f\in\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

(1)

We have the equivalence
\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\asymp_{s,\kappa}\sqrt{\lVert\Uppi_{\mathrm{L}}^{\kappa}f\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+\lVert\Uppi_{\mathrm{H}}^{\kappa}f\rVert^{2}_{H^{s}}}
.

(2)

We have that
\Uppi_{\mathrm{L}}^{\kappa}f\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with the estimates
\lVert\Uppi_{\mathrm{L}}^{\kappa}f\rVert_{W^{k,\infty}}\lesssim_{k,\kappa}\lVert\Uppi_{\mathrm{L}}^{\kappa}f\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

This is Theorem 5.5 in Leoni and Tice
[
38
]
.
∎

Our next result, which is Proposition B.2 in Stevenson and Tice
[
56
]
, enumerates important algebra properties of the anisotropic Sobolev spaces. In what follows, we denote

(A.1.8)

\mathcal{H}^{0}_{(\kappa)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})=\{{f\in\mathcal{H}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\;\mathrm{supp}\mathscr{F}[f]\subseteq\overline{B(0,\kappa)}}\}.

Proposition A.4

(Algebra properties of anisotropic Sobolev spaces)
.

Suppose that
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{H}^{0}_{(\kappa)}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for some
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. The following hold.

(1)

The product
f_{1}f_{2}
belongs to
\mathcal{H}_{(2\kappa)}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and satisfies the estimate
\lVert f_{1}f_{2}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim_{\kappa}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}
.

(2)

Set

(A.1.9)

r_{d}=\begin{cases}1+\big\lfloor\frac{d+1}{d-1}\big\rfloor,&\text{if }d>1,\\
1&\text{if }d=1.\end{cases}

Assume additionally that
f_{3},\dots,f_{r_{d}}\in\mathcal{H}_{(\kappa)}^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then the pointwise product
\prod_{j=1}^{r_{d}}f_{j}
belongs to
(L^{2}\cap\mathcal{H}^{0}_{(r_{d}\cdot\kappa)})(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and satisfies

(A.1.10)

\Big\lVert\prod_{j=1}^{r_{d}}f_{j}\Big\rVert_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{\kappa,d}\prod_{j=1}^{r_{d}}\lVert f_{j}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}.

(3)

If
g\in\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then the pointwise product
f_{1}g
belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and satisfies

(A.1.11)

\lVert f_{1}g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\lesssim_{\kappa,s}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lVert g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}.

Corollary A.5

(More algebra properties)
.

The following hold for
s\geqslant 1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor
,
t\leqslant s
, and
g\in\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

(1)

If
f\in H^{t}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then the pointwise product
fg
belongs to
H^{t}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and obeys the estimate

(A.1.12)

\lVert fg\rVert_{H^{t}}\lesssim\lVert f\rVert_{H^{t}}\lVert g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }t\leqslant 1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert f\rVert_{H^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}\lVert g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{t}}&\text{if }t>1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor.\end{cases}.

(2)

If
h\in\mathcal{H}^{t}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then the pointwise product
gh
belongs to
\mathcal{H}^{t}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and obeys the estimate

(A.1.13)

\lVert hg\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{t}}\lesssim\lVert h\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{t}}\lVert g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}+\begin{cases}0&\text{if }t\leqslant 1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor,\\
\lVert h\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}\lVert g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{t}}&\text{if }t>1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor\end{cases}

(3)

If
g\in\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then
g^{r_{d}}
, for
r_{d}
as in (
A.1.9
), belongs to
H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and obeys the estimate

(A.1.14)

\lVert g^{r_{d}}\rVert_{H^{s}}\lesssim\lVert g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{s}}\lVert g\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{1+\lfloor d/2\rfloor}}^{r_{d}-1}.

Proof.

For the first item we decompose
g=\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{L}}g+\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{H}}g
. For the low piece
f\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{L}}g
, we use the second item of Proposition
A.3
along with the fourth item of Proposition
A.23
. For the high piece
f\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{H}}g
, we instead use the first and second items of the two aforementioned propositions, respectively.

For the second item, we decompose both
g
and
h
into high and low pieces via
\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{L}}
and
\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{H}}
and inspect the four terms in the expanded product individually. For either of the mixed high-low terms, we invoke the third item of Proposition
A.4
. For the low-low term, we instead use the first item. Finally, for the high-high term, we use the first item of Proposition
A.3
along with the second item of Proposition
A.23
.

For the third item, we use the binomial theorem to write

(A.1.15)

g^{r_{d}}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{r_{d}}\binom{r_{d}}{\ell}(\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{L}}g)^{\ell}(\Uppi^{1}_{\mathrm{H}}g)^{r_{d}-\ell}.

The terms indexed with
\ell<r_{d}
are handled via the first and second items of this result. The
\ell=r_{d}
term is handled via the second item of Proposition
A.4
.
∎

A.2.
Tame structure abstraction

The goal of this sub-appendix is to review notions of Banach scales and tameness of mappings between them. This is a rapid treatment of the thorough development of Section 2.1 in Stevenson and Tice
[
56
]
.

Definition A.6
.

Given
N\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{{\infty}\}
we define
\llbracket N\rrbracket\subseteq\mathbb{N}
to be the set
\llbracket N\rrbracket=\{{n\in\mathbb{N}\;:\;n\leqslant N}\}
.

Definition A.7

(Banach scales)
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}^{+}\cup\{{\infty}\}
and let
\mathbf{E}=\{E^{s}\}_{s\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}
be a collection of Banach spaces over a common field, either
\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{C}
.

(1)

We say that
\mathbf{E}
is a Banach scale if for each
s\in\llbracket N-1\rrbracket
we have the non-expansive inclusion
E^{s+1}\hookrightarrow E^{s}
, i.e.
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{E^{s}}\leqslant\lVert\cdot\rVert_{E^{s+1}}
.

(2)

If
\mathbf{E}
is a Banach scale, then we define the scale’s terminal space to be
E^{N}=\bigcap_{s\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}E^{s}
and endow it with the Fréchet topology induced by the collection of norms
\{\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{E^{s}}\}_{s\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}
. Note that if
N<\infty
, then
E^{N}
has the standard Banach topology from its norm.

(3)

If
\mathbf{E}
is a Banach scale, then we write
B_{E^{r}}(u,\delta)\subseteq E^{r}
for the
E^{r}
-open ball of radius
\delta>0
, centered at
u\in E^{r}
.

(4)

If
\mathbf{E}
is a Banach scale and
E^{N}
is dense in
E^{s}
for each
s\in\llbracket N\rrbracket
, then we say that
\mathbf{E}
is terminally dense.

Remark A.8

(Products of Banach scales)
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{E}_{i}=\{E^{s}_{i}\}_{s\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}
is a Banach scale for
i\in\{1,\dotsc,n\}
, each over the same field. Then
\mathbf{F}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{E}_{i}=\{\prod_{i=1}^{n}E^{s}_{i}\}_{s\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}
is a Banach scale when each
\prod_{i=1}^{n}E^{s}_{i}
is endowed with the norm

(A.2.1)

\left\lVert u_{1},\dotsc,u_{n}\right\rVert_{\prod_{i=1}^{n}E^{s}_{i}}=\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\lVert u_{i}\right\rVert_{E^{s}_{i}}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.

Definition A.9

(Tame maps)
.

Let
\mathbf{E}=\{{E^{s}}\}_{s\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}
and
\mathbf{F}=\{{F^{s}}\}_{s\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}
be Banach scales over the same field,
\mu,r\in\llbracket N\rrbracket
with
\mu\leqslant r
,
U\subseteq E^{r}
be an open set, and
P:U\to F^{0}
.

(1)

We say that
P
satisfies tame estimates of order
\mu
and base
r
(with respect to the Banach scales
\mathbf{E}
and
\mathbf{F}
) if for all
\llbracket N\rrbracket\ni s\geqslant r
, there exists a constant
C_{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that for all
f\in U\cap E^{s}
we have the inclusion
P(f)\in F^{s-\mu}
as well as the estimate

(A.2.2)

\lVert P(f)\rVert_{F^{s-\mu}}\leqslant C_{s}\langle\lVert f\rVert_{E^{s}}\rangle.

(2)

We say that
P
is
\mu
-tame with base
r
if for each
f\in U
, there exists an
E^{r}
-open set
V\subseteq U
such that
f\in V
and the restricted map
P\restriction V:V\to F^{0}
satisfies tame estimates of order
\mu
and base
r
.

(3)

We say that
P
is strongly
\mu
-tame with base
r
if on every
E^{r}
-open and bounded subset
V\subseteq U
, the restricted map
P\restriction V
satisfies tame estimates of order
\mu
and base
r
.

(4)

We say that
P
is (strongly)
\mu
-tamely
C^{0}
with base
r
if
P
is (strongly)
\mu
-tame with base
r
and if for every
\llbracket N\rrbracket\ni s\geqslant r
we have that
P:U\cap E^{s}\to F^{s-\mu}

is continuous as a map from
E^{s}
to
F^{s-\mu}
. The collections of such maps will be denoted by
T^{0}_{\mu,r}(U,\mathbf{E};\mathbf{F})
and
{{}_{\mathrm{s}}}T^{0}_{\mu,r}(U,\mathbf{E};\mathbf{F})
, respectively.

(5)

For
k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
we say that
P
is (strongly)
\mu
-tamely
C^{k}
with base
r
if for all
\llbracket N\rrbracket\ni s\geqslant r
the map
P:U\cap E^{s}\to F^{s-\mu}
is
C^{k}
and for all
j\in\{{0,1,\dots,k}\}
the
j^{\mathrm{th}}
derivative map, thought of as mapping
D^{j}P:U\times\prod_{\ell=1}^{j}E^{r}\to F^{0}
, is (strongly)
\mu
-tame with base
r
with respect to the Banach scales
\mathbf{E}^{1+j}
and
\mathbf{F}
. In other words,
D^{j}P\in T^{0}_{\mu,r}(U\times\prod_{\ell=1}^{j}E^{r},\mathbf{E}^{1+j};\mathbf{F})
or, in the strong case,
D^{j}P\in{{}_{\mathrm{s}}}T^{0}_{\mu,r}(U\times\prod_{\ell=1}^{j}E^{r},\mathbf{E}^{1+j};\mathbf{F})
. The collections of such maps will be denoted by
T^{k}_{\mu,r}(U,\mathbf{E};\mathbf{F})
and
{{}_{\mathrm{s}}}T^{k}_{\mu,r}(U,\mathbf{E};\mathbf{F})
, respectively.

(6)

The collections of (strongly)
\mu
-tamely smooth with base
r
maps are
T^{\infty}_{\mu,r}(U,\mathbf{E};\mathbf{F})=\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}}T^{k}_{\mu,r}(U,\mathbf{E};\mathbf{F})
and
{{}_{\mathrm{s}}}T^{\infty}_{\mu,r}(U,\mathbf{E};\mathbf{F})=\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}}{{}_{\mathrm{s}}}T^{k}_{\mu,r}(U,\mathbf{E};\mathbf{F})
.

(7)

When
U=E^{r}
we will often use the abbreviated notation
T^{k}_{\mu,r}(\mathbf{E},\mathbf{F})
and
{{}_{\mathrm{s}}}T^{k}_{\mu,r}(\mathbf{E};\mathbf{F})
in place of
T^{k}_{\mu,r}(E^{r},\mathbf{E};\mathbf{F})
and
{{}_{\mathrm{s}}}T^{k}_{\mu,r}(U,\mathbf{E};\mathbf{F})
, respectively.

The following result, which is proved in Lemma 2.5 in Stevenson and Tice
[
56
]
, is a useful characterization of tameness when there is multilinear structure present in the map.

Lemma A.10

(Multilinearity and tame maps)
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and
r,\mu\in\llbracket N\rrbracket
with
\mu\leqslant r
. Let
\mathbf{F}=\{{F^{s}}\}_{s\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}
,
\mathbf{G}=\{{G^{s}}\}_{s\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}
, and
\mathbf{E}_{j}=\{{E^{s}_{j}}\}_{s\in\llbracket N\rrbracket}
, for
j\in\{{1,\dots,k}\}
